cameras tracking innocent people, enforcement of victimless
crimes—these violate privacy rights. Privacy isn't a privilege                         @AKLibertarians
police grant; it's yours by birthright.

Self-Defense

1980s law for pouring a free drink. Selective enforcement
serves power, not justice.

       STEWARDSHIP FOR ALASKA,
             BY ALASKA
         Fifty years ago, Alaskans chose to save a portion of the state’s resource wealth and invest
                   it for future generations. That vote created the Alaska Permanent Fund.


                                                                                                                  THE 1976 VOTE
                                                                                                              75,588 YES | 38,518 NO
                                                                                                                             Generations of Value



                               ALASKA CONSTITUTION ARTICLE IX, SECTION 15
                                                                                                               Alaska Permanent Fund
  At least twenty-five percent of all mineral lease rentals, royalties, royalty sale proceeds, federal
    mineral revenue sharing payments and bonuses received by the State shall be placed in a
permanent fund, the principal of which shall be used only for those income-producing investments
  specifically designated by law as eligible for permanent fund investments. All income from the
     permanent fund shall be deposited in the general fund unless otherwise provided by law.


                           ALASKANS CAN TAKE PRIDE IN WHAT THEY BUILT
  The Alaska Permanent Fund was created to convert a portion of Alaska’s finite resource wealth
     into a lasting financial resource for generations of Alaskans. The initial royalty deposit of
       $734,000 has grown into a public asset valued at $91.9 billion as of June 30, 2026.
       The Fund supports dividends, public services, and Alaska’s long-term financial future.

BUILT FROM ALASKA’S RESOURCES. INVESTED FOR ALASKA’S FUTURE.
For 50 years, the Fund has fulfilled a simple but powerful purpose: saving, investing, and growing
Alaska’s resource wealth for the benefit of current and future generations.

$59.4B              Resource revenues from oil, gas, and mining $20.8B, along with inflation proofing
                    and other appropriations $38.6B from the Legislature, are deposited into the
                    constitutionally protected Principal and invested in a globally diversified portfolio
                    designed to generate long-term returns.

$14.6B              Realized investment earnings are deposited into the Earnings Reserve Account,
                    from which they can be legislatively appropriated to provide inflation protection for
                    the Principal and to support the annual Percent of Market Value (POMV) draw. In
                    FY26, the POMV draw of $3.8B accounted for nearly 60% of Alaska’s unrestricted
                    general fund revenue, more than twice the share from oil and gas.
$17.9B              Unrealized appreciation in the Principal $14.4B and Earnings Reserve Account
                    $3.5B reflects changes in asset value.

INVESTED GLOBALLY. MANAGED FOR GENERATIONS.
The Alaska Permanent Fund is managed as a globally diversified investment portfolio, invested
across eight asset classes in public and private markets and designed to maximize long-term,
risk-adjusted returns for current and future generations of Alaskans.
Over the past 50 years, the Fund has evolved with global investment markets, broadening its
investment opportunities, strengthening resilience, and improving its ability to preserve and
grow Alaska’s resource wealth over time.
Through disciplined stewardship and professional investment management, APFC helps convert
a portion of Alaska’s finite natural resource wealth into lasting financial value for generations.




  ALASKA’S PERMANENT FUND | Fifty Years. Generations of Value.                                                                                       3
                                                                         PAGE 148                                                            2026 REGION I
                  INVESTMENT EARNINGS AT WORK FOR ALASKA
                                     Supporting Today and Strengthening Tomorrow


                                                   TOTAL RETURN
                                                      Since Inception
                                        $119,861,000,000

    PAID TO CURRENT GENERATIONS                                            FOR FUTURE GENERATIONS
           42.9% | $51.4B                                                     57.1% | $68.4B

     Over 50 years, $20.8 billion in resource revenue deposits from oil, gas and mining have been
transformed through disciplined investing into $119.8 billion in cumulative investment earnings. This
  demonstrates that the Fund’s value has been driven primarily by investment growth—not deposits
alone. Since inception, 42.9% of those earnings have benefited current generations through dividends
 and public services. The remaining 57.1% has stayed invested or been appropriated to the Principal
    for inflation protection, helping preserve and grow the Fund for future generations of Alaskans.




    The choice Alaskans made in 1976 created lasting value that continues to pay dividends
       for Alaskans and support public services today. In its first 50 years, the Fund has:

    • Transformed a portion of Alaska’s oil                     • Replaced traditional oil and gas revenues
      wealth into a permanent financial asset                     as Alaska’s primary source of undesignated
      to strengthen Alaska’s financial future.                    general fund revenue through annual draws
    • Grown from its first deposits to more                       from the Fund.
      than $91.9 billion through disciplined,                   • Benefited Alaskans today while preserving
      long-term stewardship.                                      value for tomorrow.




                                       INVESTING FOR ALASKA
          Built from Alaska’s natural resources and guided by a generational perspective, the
           Alaska Permanent Fund is invested to benefit Alaskans today and into the future.

   The Fund’s balance sheet and income statement are included in the Official Elections Pamphlet as required by AS 37.13.170

                   ALASKA PUBLIC OFFICES COMMISSION


WHY DOES APOC EXIST?
The mission of APOC is to encourage the public’s confidence in their elected and appointed
public officials by administering and enforcing Alaska’s disclosure statutes and publishing
financial information about election campaigns, public officials, lobbyists and lobbyist
employers.

LAWS ADMINISTERED BY APOC
APOC administers and enforces four disclosure laws; AS 15.13 Campaign Disclosure, AS
24.45 Lobbying, AS 39.50 Public Official Financial Disclosure, and AS 24.60 Legislative
Financial Disclosure. These laws require the disclosure of information that enables citizens
to trace the influence of private interests on public decision-making. This disclosure helps to
limit privileged influence through revealing special interests and exposing conflicts of interest.
The laws are designed to develop accountability in government by giving the public access
to information to judge the credentials and performance of public officials and candidates for
public offices. The disclosure reports filed with APOC are public records.

WHO IS THE COMMISSION?
The two political parties or political groups with the largest number of registered voters at the
time of the most recent gubernatorial election each nominate two commission members. The
four members nominate a fifth member from applicants from the general public. The governor
appoints and the legislature confirms the nominations. Terms are for five years, with a
different term expiring each year. The Commission currently consists of two Republican Party
members, two Democratic Party members, and one public member.

HOW CAN WE HELP YOU?
This agency is the public’s source of information on all aspects of the disclosure laws. We are
committed to fulfilling our mission by providing the public with information. Most of the
information can be accessed through APOC’s website. You can find out who has contributed
to a particular ballot group, which political action committees are spending the most, which
lobbyists are working for which employers, who is contributing to candidates and how much
is being given and spent to influence voters. APOC works with the public and those who file
financial reports to make the disclosure process effective, efficient, and transparent.

Visit our website or contact our office for information about how to locate the information
required by the laws that APOC administers. This is the public’s agency and we welcome the
opportunity to make APOC work for you.
